  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- v18: v5 + try to reduce the carry chain in the subtractor\n// The key insight: e_norm = lhs - limit_under = (exp_sum + 1 + carry) - (15 + lz)\n// We can rewrite as: e_norm = exp_sum - 14 - lz + carry\n// This separates the +carry from the main subtractor, allowing exp_sum-14-lz\n// to be computed in parallel with the carry path\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_shr_grd = (sr == 0) ? {PROD_W{1'b0}} : (prod >> (sr - 5'd1));\n  wire guard_n = prod_shr_grd[0];\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below_shr_stk = (sr <= 5'd1) ? {PROD_W{1'b0}} : (below >> (sr - 5'd2));\n  wire sticky_n = below_shr_stk[0];\n  wire round_up = guard_n & (sticky_n | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // Exponent: e_norm = (exp_sum + 1 + carry) - (15 + lz)\n  // = exp_sum - 14 - lz + carry\n  // Compute base = exp_sum - 14 (subtract constant), then subtract lz and add carry\n  // Actually, let's try: e_base = exp_sum - 14, then e_norm = e_base - lz + carry\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  // e_base = exp_sum - 14 (8-bit, exp_sum >= 2 so e_base >= -12, range -12..48)\n  wire [7:0] e_base      = exp_sum - 8'd14;\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};  // 15 + lz\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + 8'd30 + {3'd0, lz};  // 45 + lz\n  // lhs = e_base - lz + carry + 1... wait, this doesn't simplify\n  // Let's just keep the original\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // Subnormal result\n  wire signed [6:0] total_shift = $signed({1'b0, 8'd26}) - $signed({2'b00, exp_sum});\n  wire [4:0] ts_abs = total_shift[4:0];\n  wire ts_neg = total_shift[6];\n  wire ts_ge0 = ~ts_neg;\n  wire ts_lt22 = ts_ge0 & (total_shift < 7'sd22);\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = ts_lt22 ? ## Summary

### Best Design
`design_v5.v` — cost **141061** (area=108.75, delay=1297ps), down from the starting point's 145429 (~3% improvement).

### What Worked Best
1. **Replacing `integer`-typed `bit_at` function with barrel-shifter logic** (v5): The biggest win was replacing the `bit_at(prod, sr-1)` function (which used 32-bit `integer` indexing) with direct barrel-shifter expressions like `prod >> (sr - 5'd1)`. This cut delay from 1338→1297ps and area slightly, yielding the best result.

2. **`exp_fin`/`frac_fin` two-level mux in packing** (v9/v11): Restructuring the output mux to first select norm vs sub, then select vs special cases, kept area neutral but didn't hurt.

### What Didn't Work / Caused Regressions
- **Tree-based if-else LZC** (v2, v8): Consistently worse than the simple loop-based LZC — the synthesis tool optimizes the loop better.
- **Combined exponent subtraction** (v6, v10, v12): Computing `e_norm = exp_sum + carry - 14 - lz` in one step was consistently worse than the original separate `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry` then `e_norm = lhs - limit_under`. The tool prefers separate add/subtract stages.
- **Narrower arithmetic** (6-bit, 7-bit in v7, v14, v16): Worse than 8-bit — the synthesis tool maps 8-bit adders to more efficient cells.
- **Removing `sub_is_zero`** (v17): Increased area slightly (109.8 vs 108.8), net worse.
- **High `target_delay`** (2000ps, 5000ps): Increased delay significantly with minimal area savings — bad for area×delay.

### Lessons Learned
1. The synthesis tool (Yosys+ASAP7) is highly sensitive to RTL structure — combining arithmetic operations that look simpler in RTL often synthesizes worse, as the tool optimizes separate stages better.
2. **`integer` types are expensive** — always use explicitly-sized wires for shift amounts and indices.
3. The default `target_delay=500ps` is near-optimal for this design; the tool can't meet it but produces the best area×delay tradeoff.
4. The critical path (exponent add→subtract→mux chain, ~1297ps) is hard to reduce further without fundamentally restructuring the algorithm (e.g., pipelining, which isn't allowed for combinational logic).
5. The reference implementation's cascade priority encoder for LZC and guard/sticky selection is not better than the simple loop — the tool handles loops well.
